﻿# Iteration Log - Void Runner

One entry per iteration. Format:
## I<nn> - <title>
- Story: ST-xxx | Goal: ...
- Changed: files
- Verification: test result / build / browser smoke
- Result: DONE / IN PROGRESS / BLOCKED
- Risk/follow-up: ...

(Entries appended below as work proceeds.)

## I01 - Project scaffold + toolchain (ST-001)
- Goal: repo builds, serves, tests run; renderer boots.
- Changed: package.json, index.html, vite-free static toolchain (scripts/serve.mjs, scripts/build.mjs, scripts/run-tests.mjs, scripts/vendor.mjs), src/main.js (scene+camera+PolarGridHelper arena), vendor/three (import map), AGENTS.md, README.md, CHANGELOG.md, public/favicon.svg
- Notes: sandbox blocks child_process spawn -> replaced Vite/esbuild with pure-JS import-map toolchain; npm install --ignore-scripts; in-process node --test runner.
- Verification: `npm test` 1/1 pass; `npm run build` exit 0; browser http://localhost:5173 console clean (favicon 404 fixed), screenshot .evidence/i01-smoke.png shows arena.
- Result: DONE
- Follow-up: pin three version after vendor (done: 0.160.1 via vendor script).

## I27 - WebAudio synth SFX (ST-027)
- Goal: asset-free sound: every gameplay event gets a short synthesized cue (oscillator + noise envelopes), initialized on first user gesture per autoplay policy, honoring saved volume/sfx + the M-key mute.
- Changed: src/render/audio.js (NEW: createAudio() - 8 cues {shoot, kill, hit, powerup, shieldPop, bomb, wave, gameover} as type/freq-sweep/dur/gain specs with per-cue noise tails; shared 0.5s white-noise buffer; master gain with setTargetAtTime smoothing; per-cue rate limiting via ctx.currentTime to avoid overlap at RAPID fire; played{} probe counters; all calls safe no-ops before init), src/main.js (SFX_CUES event->cue map in the drain loop, gesture listeners pointerdown/keydown {once} -> markAudioReady -> audio.init + setMuted(muted), M key forwards to audio.setMuted, applySettings forwards volume/sfx once audio exists, __vr += audio/markAudioReady).
- Verification: npm test 111/111 pass (browser-layer feature; core unchanged); build exit 0; browser: state 'uninitialized' pre-gesture -> 'running' after markAudioReady; gameplay events scheduled cues (fire->shoot, kill->kill, hit->hit, gameover->gameover observed via played counters across forced kills/hits/game-overs); all 8 cue specs played directly with zero exceptions; setVolume(0.3)/setMuted(true/false) round-trip; console 0 errors. ## I29 - audio polish: compressor, stereo pan, pitch jitter, stingers (ST-029)
- Goal: make the synth layer feel less flat and survive stacked cues: a master compressor, world-x stereo pan, per-play pitch jitter, and real multi-note stingers for wave-clear and game-over.
- Changed: src/render/audio.js (master chain now gain -> DynamicsCompressor[-18/20/6/0.003/0.25] -> destination; every play routes through a per-cue StereoPanner with pan = clamp(x/26,-1..1)*0.8; scheduleNote adds +-3% pitch jitter so repeated cues never sound looped; CUES gains note arrays - cleared = rising 523/659/784 sine arp, gameover = falling 440/330/196 saw motif with noise tail; sfx(name, pos) takes an optional position, lastPan() probe added), src/main.js (SFX_CUES maps waveCleared->cleared, drain passes e.data.pos when present).
- Verification: npm test 111/111 pass (browser-layer; core unchanged); build exit 0; browser: all 9 cues (incl. both stingers) scheduled with zero exceptions; pan probes with real waits -> left -0.615, center 0, clamped +0.8 (matches clamp(x/26)*0.8); full chain live: shatter field -> waveCleared +1, cleared cue +1, banner "WAVE 1 CLEARED", state -> intermission; console 0 errors. Self-review caught + fixed a swapped gain/time arg in the stinger scheduling loop.
- Result: DONE
- Follow-up: I30 floating score popups (+combo) at kill position.

## I30 - floating score popups on kill (ST-030)
- Goal: per-kill score feedback - a "+N" popup floating up at the kill position; combo-multiplier kills (x2+) show large in gold; reduced motion shows a fade in place instead of a float.
- Changed: src/render/scorePopups.js (NEW: pool of 12 fixed DOM divs; add(x,y,text,{big}) starts a 0.9s popup and recycles the oldest when the pool is exhausted; sync(dt, motionOff) ages each active popup, reprojects world (x, 0.5, y) through the live camera every frame (explicit updateMatrixWorld + matrixWorldInverse so it tracks the follow-camera and shake), hides when behind the camera (z>1), floats up 40px over the life (skipped under reduced motion) and fades over the last 45%; clear() empties the pool), src/main.js (popups created with body + camera, drain loop kill event -> popups.add(pos, '+points', big = multiplier >= 2), syncVisuals calls popups.sync after follow-camera + shake, Enter start/restart clears stale popups, __vr += popups).

- Goal: the game-over screen shows the full session summary - kills, max combo, time survived (m:ss), and the wave reached - so a run is readable at a glance (story table ST-031 row).

- Verification: npm test 115/115 pass (4 new: kills counted; reset clears kills+maxCombo; world kills tracked across 3 bullet kills; world time freezes after game over); build exit 0; browser: 3-kill session -> game over showed "KILLS 3 WAVE 1" and "MAX COMBO x3 TIME 0:00"; a death before wave 1 started correctly shows WAVE 1 (not 0); restart (Enter) resets kills/maxCombo/time to 0; .evidence/i31-gameover-stats.png captured the full stats block; console 0 errors.

- Changed: src/render/tutorialHint.js (NEW: single fixed DOM overlay, z-index 9, 6s lifetime with 2s fade-out tail, reduced motion uses hard on/off; show()/hide()/sync(dt, motionOff)), src/main.js (hint created, Enter start/restart shows it alongside the popup clear, syncVisuals calls hint.sync after popups, __vr += hint).

P PAUSE"); opacity 1 at start, ~0.49 at t=5s (fade tail), 0 at t=7s; reduced-motion path holds 1 until the lifetime end (hard off); .evidence/i32-tutorial-hint.png captured the live hint in-wave-1; console 0 errors.

## I33 - touch controls (ST-033)
- Goal: playable on touch devices with the story scheme - drag = thrust, tap = fire toward tap, two-finger tap = pause.
- Changed: src/render/input.js (rewritten around pointer events: touch Map tracks active fingers; a single finger drag > 24px sets state.thrust to the normalized drag direction (screen right/down = sim +x/+y) and touches fire; aim follows the finger via NDC; releasing the last finger starts a 0.22s fire tail so a short tap still launches at least one bullet (fire interval ~0.18s); second finger down fires a cooldown-gated (0.45s) onPause callback; tick(dt) decays fire tail + cooldown; keyboard/mouse behavior unchanged; pointercancel/blur clear all touches), src/main.js (createInput({ onPause: togglePause }) with togglePause extracted from the P/Esc handler; resolveAimPoint aims along the drag 10 units ahead of the ship while touchDrag is active (fire forward, not behind the finger); tick() calls input.tick(dt) when running on live input; __vr unchanged - input already exposed).
- Verification: npm test 115/115 pass (render layer; core unchanged); build exit 0; browser with synthetic pointerType:"touch" PointerEvents: drag 200px/-100px -> thrust (0.894, -0.447) == touchDrag, shipDelta (7.28, -3.64) moves 2:1 along the drag; tap release -> firing stays true (fire tail) and >=1 bullet launched (eventTally.fire delta >= 1), firing false after tail decay; two-finger down -> PAUSED, second two-finger (post-cooldown) -> PLAYING; keyboard pause path still uses the same togglePause; console 0 errors. Note: touch UI is input-only (no on-screen controls) per the story; desktop behavior unaffected.
- Result: DONE
- Follow-up: I34 balance pass 1 (ST-034: review numbers vs GDD, adjust fire/asteroid/drop pacing, document rationale in balance-notes).
## I34 - balance pass 1 + spawn safety (ST-034)
- Goal: first balance pass vs GDD - fix the unfair rim-spawn case and tune the two pacing levers with documented rationale.
- Changed: src/core/config.js (combo.window 3.5 -> 4.0; powerup.dropChance 0.22 -> 0.25; NEW wave.spawnMinPlayerDist = 8), src/core/asteroids.js (spawn(size, speedMult, at, noNear) - rim spawns re-roll their angle up to 6x while closer than noNear.min to the player; falls back to the first placement so the concurrent cap keeps priority; explicit-at spawns (splits) untouched; RNG consumption unchanged when noNear is null), src/core/waves.js (step(dt, playerPos) threads a noNear {x, y, min: wave.spawnMinPlayerDist} into field.spawn), src/core/game.js (waves.step receives this.ship.pos), design/gdd.md (authority updated: combo decays after 4s x2, drop chance 25%), production/balance-notes.md (I34 rationale + documented plateaus: concurrent cap 24 from wave 9, speed 1.9 from wave 13), tests/asteroids.test.js (new deterministic test, RNG seed 35: 10 L spawns with a player at the +x rim all land >= 8 units out).
- Verification: npm test 116/116 pass (+1 new); build exit 0; browser smoke: ship parked at (24,0) hugging the rim, forced wave 1 with 12 queued - 8 spawned (wave-1 concurrent limit 6+2*1=8, 4 queued correctly) and min rock-ship distance 10.5 >= 8 after 2s of drift; console 0 errors; screenshot .evidence/i34-spawn-safety.png.

## I35 - bloom post-fx, High quality (ST-035)
- Goal: deliver the GDD-pledged bloom ("FX quality High/Low - Low disables bloom + reduces particles", GDD line 74) - bright game elements glow on High, Low falls back to the direct render path.
- Changed: src/core/config.js (NEW fx.bloom { strength 0.6, radius 0.35, threshold 0.72 }), src/render/bloom.js (NEW module: EffectComposer + RenderPass + UnrealBloomPass + OutputPass; setEnabled toggle; resize() syncs pixel ratio + size; render() uses composer when enabled, else renderer.render), src/main.js (createBloom after camera; applySettings wires bloom.setEnabled(fx === high) - so the existing settings panel + storage persist the choice; resize handler calls bloom.resize; frame() renders via bloom.render(); __vr exposes bloom). No new downloads - the full three.js addons tree (r160) was already vendored at vendor/jsm including postprocessing.
- Verification: npm test 117/117 pass (+1 config bloom sanity test: strength/radius positive finite, threshold in [0,1]); build exit 0; browser: default High -> bloom.enabled true; applySettings fx low -> false, high -> true (settings toggle path proven); gameplay frame with 8 rocks + live bullets rendered through composer, screenshot .evidence/i35-bloom-high.png shows halos on the ship glow and stars; bloom off frame .evidence/i35-bloom-off.png is sharp/dark for comparison; console 0 errors across play -> hit -> game over with the composer active.
- Result: DONE
- Follow-up: I36 perf pass + ?fps=1 meter (ST-036) - measure the bloom cost now that the composer is in the loop.
## I36 - perf pass + ?fps=1 meter (ST-036)
- Goal: measure the post-bloom frame cost with a built-in meter, and remove the per-frame waste found in the hot paths.
- Changed: src/render/fpsMeter.js (NEW: opt-in div meter, 0.5s averaging window, change-cached text; ?fps=1 shows fps + ms, ?fps=2 adds draw calls + triangles), src/render/particles.js (live-particle counter: sync() early-returns with zero GPU attribute uploads when no particle is alive - the menu/wave-break case; burst() increments on parked-slot overwrite, death decrements; exposes get live()), src/main.js (meter wired: created only when the ?fps param is present, sync called at end of syncVisuals, exposed on __vr; renderer.info.autoReset = false + manual info.reset() between the stats read and the render so composer multi-pass frames accumulate one true per-frame count instead of per-pass; hud.render/screens.update now reuse hoisted opts objects instead of allocating two per frame).
- Verification: npm test 117/117 (core unchanged; render layer); build exit 0; browser ?fps=2: menu 60fps/16.6ms; wave 4 with 20 rocks + firing + bloom: 56-60fps, 16.7-17.8ms, 40 calls, 1638 tris (scene ~20 meshes + ~20 bloom-pass quads - the composer cost is now visible and is the standing reason for the later adaptive-quality iteration); particles live counter 0 -> 40 (burst) -> 0 (decay) proving the dirty-flag path; screenshots .evidence/i36-fps-meter-load.png + load2.png; console 0 errors. Bug caught during this iteration: with default info.autoReset the meter only saw the last composer pass (calls 1) - fixed via autoReset=false + per-frame reset after the read.
- Result: DONE
- Follow-up: I37 bugfix A (ST-037).
## I37 - bugfix A: stray Enter mid-game (ST-037)
- Goal: bugfix iteration - hunt real defects in the session-key / touch / core boundary space and fix the first confirmed one with a repro.
- Bug: the Enter keydown handler ran its session side effects (newBest=false, popups.clear(), hint.show()) BEFORE the state check. Repro: start game, let the 6s tutorial hint fade, press Enter mid-game -> the tutorial hint instantly re-appears (opacity 0 -> 1, full 6s overlay) and any live score popups are wiped, while the game keeps running (state stayed PLAYING, no restart - the harmful part was the UX spam). Root cause: side effects outside the MENU/GAME_OVER branches.
- Changed: src/main.js (Enter handler: newBest reset + popups.clear() + hint.show() now only inside the MENU->begin and GAME_OVER->restart branches; stray Enter in PLAYING/PAUSED is a no-op. Also removed a stray double semicolon after audio.setMuted in the M handler).
- Verification: browser repro before fix (mid-game Enter flipped hint opacity 0->1 at PLAYING); after fix, dedicated 9s-fade probe: hint opacity 0 -> stray Enter -> 0, state stays PLAYING (bugFixed=true); regression paths intact - MENU Enter starts game with hint shown (opacity 1), GAME_OVER Enter restarts with score 0 and hint shown; npm test 117/117; build exit 0; console 0 errors. Also probed and cleared (no fix needed): knockback ship-arena clamp (r=25.10 + radius 0.9 <= 26), restart state hygiene (score/combo/kills/wave/time/drops all reset), combo timer freeze on pause (correct: 6 paused sim-seconds did not decay the chain), homing NaN paths (v2Norm zero-vector guarded with l||1; atan2(0,0)=0), one-hit-per-frame ship collision (single best hit resolved), touch second-finger bookkeeping.
- Result: DONE
- Follow-up: I38 code review pass (ST-038).

## I39 - balance pass 2: late-game speed ramp (ST-039)
- Goal: balance iteration - extend difficulty past the wave-13 plateau where the speed ramp hit its cap, as the documented counterweight to the I34 sustain buffs, and close the open I21/I23/I34 tuning revisit notes with analysis.

- Verification: node scripts/run-tests.mjs 117/117 (the waves cap test failed once against the new cap, proving the probe was tied to the old number - fixed symbolically, not by weakening the assertion); build exit 0; browser live probe of the running ramp: wave 13 -> 1.78, wave 18 -> 2.08, wave 19 -> 2.1, wave 30 -> 2.1 (exactly the designed curve; waves 14-18 were flat 1.9 before this change); 0 console errors.

Suite B executed live in browser, all 8 steps pass: 1) Enter -> PLAYING + hint shown, 2) live-intent fire -> 2 bullets + fire events, 3) bullet kill -> +160 score (exactly 50x1.0 + 50x1.1: the combo multiplier math verified in-situ) + kill event, 4) rapid/shield/magnet all active after forced apply, 5) P -> PAUSED with sim time frozen over 1s of advance, P -> PLAYING, 6) hit at 1 life -> GAME_OVER + best persisted, 7) Enter -> restart with score 0 / lives 3 / wave 0. ## I42 - wave stall advance (ST-042, bugfix B)
- Goal: close playtest finding F1 - the top design gap (a wave that is never cleared never advances; Run A camped wave 1 for 180s at 1 hit) - with a config-driven, deterministic stall advance.
- Changed: src/core/waves.js (waveElapsed stall timer: counts active time only, reset on wave start / clear / stall; at wave.stallTime the wave advances regardless - toSpawn set for the new wave, leftover rocks carry (not deleted, not sped up), no intermission; ev.stallAdvance added, ev.startedWave reused so the "WAVE n" banner flow is unchanged). src/core/config.js (wave.stallTime: 45). design/gdd.md ("Waves & Difficulty" advance rule: + stall advance, carryover, skipped intermission). tests/waves.test.js (+3: stall carries leftovers and resets the timer; clear-in-time never stalls; repeated stalls escalate 2->3; seeds 36-38). production/balance-notes.md (I42: 45s is ~2x the skilled early pace; density self-bounds via the concurrent cap 24; watch items I90/I92).
- Verification: node --test 120/120 green; build exit 0. Browser smoke (live dev build, new config confirmed loaded with stallTime 45): start game, wave 1 active (5 rocks), waveElapsed parked at 44s, advanced 2s -> wave advanced to 2 with the "WAVE 2" banner, 5/5 leftover rocks carried (none deleted), toSpawn set for wave 2 (6), waveElapsed reset, wave event x1 / waveCleared x0, lives intact, 0 console errors/warnings. Screenshots: .evidence/i42-stall-advance-wave2.png (mid-play: WAVE 2 banner, score 0, 10 rocks) and .evidence/i42-stall-advance.png (game over after real-time drift reached wave 2 with 0 kills - the stall path end-to-end).
- Result: DONE
- Follow-up: I43 edge cases (ST-043) - visibilitychange auto-pause, resize, WebGL context-loss recovery, #flash null guard (QA plan R7, deferred from I38).

## I64 - balance pass 3: new-content pacing (ST-064)

- Goal: balance iteration - review the pacing of all content added since balance pass 2 (wave mods, golden, near-miss, mines, Monolith boss, enemy bolts, overdrive, endless curve, vortex) against the ship's capabilities; make one bounded tuning where a real pacing gap exists and document the rest.
- Changed: src/core/config.js (boss: +hpFirstWave 10 / hpStep 12 / hpCap 88), src/core/boss.js (pure bossHpForWave(cfg, wave); Monolith constructor takes an optional HP override), src/core/game.js (spawnBossForWave threads the wave into the boss HP), tests/boss.test.js (+2 tests: the wave-HP curve incl. cap, and a live wave-15 boss built at base+step), design/gdd.md (Monolith HP note), production/balance-notes.md (I64 full pacing review + closed-with-analysis).
- Rationale: the fixed 40-HP Monolith was a trivial free +1000 by wave 15 while density/mines/vortex all ramped in endless - the 'big threat' beat was disappearing in the mode with no end. HP now climbs +12 per 5 waves from wave 10, capped at 88 (2.2x base, always beatable); speed unchanged so the boss stays always-dodgeable.
- Verification: 177/177 unit tests green (+2, core change); build exit 0. Browser: live world HP-by-wave probe -> wave 5/10 = 40, 15 = 52, 20 = 64, 30/60 = 88 (cap); 0 console errors. No rendering changed (boss HP bar shows hpFrac 0..1, unaffected by absolute HP).

## I79 - storage versioning + corruption guards (ST-079)

- Goal: make persisted state (best/settings/scores) safe against version drift and hand-edited/corrupt data - a bad payload must fall back to safe defaults, never crash, and never mis-parse a newer client's schema.
- Changed: src/core/storage.js (added pure exported migratePayload(version, data, migrations?, targetVersion?) - rejects non-object, corrupt/non-integer, and future (>target) versions, treats missing version as legacy v1, runs ordered migration steps with abort-on-error; added internal _readVersioned that validates+migrates on read and best-effort rewrites an upgraded older payload; getBest/getSettings/getHighScores now route through it; MIGRATIONS registry stub for future schema bumps). tests/storage.test.js (+13: migratePayload identity/legacy/future/corrupt/non-object/multi-step/missing-step/throwing + real getters rejecting future-versioned payloads while still reading legacy).
- Verification: node --check clean; 223/223 (13 new); build exit 0. Browser (real localStorage adapter): setSettings round-trip 0.33 + stamps version 1, future version 99 -> defaults, legacy unversioned still reads, remove -> defaults; console 0 errors/0 warnings.

## I81 - hit-stop (boss/bomb) + camera punch (ST-081)

- Goal: land the high-impact moments (boss death, bomb blast) with game-feel weight - a brief sim freeze (hit-stop) plus a directed camera punch on top of the existing shake, so the arena stops, jolts, and resumes.
- Changed: src/core/fx.js (added pure createPunch(cfg): directed exponential-decay camera-jolt envelope; offset() ADDS into out so it composes after shake, which overwrites). src/core/config.js (fx.punch {decay 12, amplitude 2.0}; fx.hitStop {bossDeath 0.09, bomb 0.07} sim-freeze seconds). src/main.js (punch instance + hitStop accumulator; bossDeath and bomb events set hitStop and trigger the punch, inside the existing !motionOff FX block (motion-gated); punch.step alongside shake.step in tick; punch.offset composed after shake.offset into the camera; frame() freezes the sim while hitStop > 0 and state is PLAYING - decrements by frameDt and clock.reset(now) discards the accumulated time so there is no catch-up burst, rendering keeps going; a freeze requested while not PLAYING is discarded). tests/fx.test.js (+5: idle no-op, max-stack/clamp trigger, zero/negative strength ignored, decay settles, exact offset math + composition with shake on the same target).
- Verification: node --check clean; 228/228 (5 new); build exit 0. Browser (real rAF loop, 60Hz, wall-vs-sim sampling): bomb -> contiguous sim plateau 0.097s (config 0.07 + frame quantization), punch magnitude exactly 0.8*exp(-12/60) = 0.655 at the first post-freeze tick; real Monolith bossDeath -> plateau 0.10s (config 0.09), punch exactly 1.0*exp(-0.2) = 0.819; boss consumed (world.boss null), game stayed PLAYING; console 0 errors/0 warnings; screenshots .evidence/i81-bossdeath-punch.png and .evidence/i81-bomb-punch.png (render healthy, no NaN).

## I82 - dynamic FOV (kick + speed) + speed-scaled look-ahead (ST-082)

- Goal: widen the camera dynamically so speed and impact read in the frame - a brief FOV kick on bomb/boss death on top of the I81 hit-stop/punch, a sustained FOV boost that scales with ship speed (speed-line feel), and a look-ahead that grows with speed so the camera leans into the motion.

## I84 - audio: master-chain pass (post-compressor makeup gain + master level-meter probe), pan math verified (ST-084)

- Goal: round out the master audio chain and verify stereo panning. The DynamicsCompressor (ST-029) already limits loud mixes but only pulls level down, so add a post-compressor makeup gain to restore perceived loudness, and add a post-makeup master level-meter probe as the foundation for the I87 sound-level pass. Re-scoped from the original 'pan by x + master compressor' because BOTH already shipped (ST-029 pan + compressor); this iteration adds the genuinely-new pieces and verifies the existing pan math in-browser.
- Changed: src/render/audio.js (master chain now master -> comp -> makeup(gain 1.4) -> destination, with a post-makeup Analyser (fftSize 1024) tapped as a sink (does not forward); new meter() reads the time-domain buffer and returns {peak, rms} in [-1,1] or null pre-init; meter exposed on the audio handle). No core/logic changes.
- Verification: node --check clean; 232/232 (render-only, count unchanged); build exit 0. Browser (gesture-initiated AudioContext, state running): pan math exact - kill@x=+13 -> lastPan +0.4 (13/26*0.8) and hit@x=-13 -> -0.4 (distinct cues so the rate-limit does not mask the flip; two back-to-back identical cues DO rate-limit before lastPan updates, confirmed by played.kill=1); meter probe live - spawning a rock then bombBlast shatters it (rocks 1->0, played.bomb=1) and the analyser reads peak 1.0226 / rms 0.3728 (non-zero, post-makeup). Observation carried to I87: an isolated loudest cue (bomb, gain 0.7) briefly pushes the post-compressor+1.4x chain to ~1.02 at the analyser (inaudible, destination still compressor-limited) - SFX weight vs headroom to revisit in the sound-level pass. Console 0 errors/0 warnings.

## I86 - per-entity particle palettes + burst shapes (ST-086)

- Goal: give each entity/event its own color signature in the particle FX and add shape variety to the bursts, so rock kills, golden pickups, near-misses, mines, boss beats, shields, ship damage, the bomb, and the overdrive pickup read differently at a glance. Every palette and shape choice is config-driven, and the burst API stays backward-compatible (a bare `color` is a one-item palette; the default shape is radial).
- Changed: src/core/config.js (NEW `fx.palette` sub-section - 10 named hex ramps keyed by entity/event: rock, golden, graze, mine, boss, bossKill, shield, hit, bomb, power; each a 2-3 color ramp; config.test.js untouched since it only checks REQUIRED top-level sections + specific numerics). src/render/particles.js (`burst()` rewritten backward-compatible - now reads `o.palette` (an array picks a color per particle via Math.random; a missing/empty palette falls back to `[o.color ?? 0xffd27a]`, so the old single-color call path is byte-for-byte equivalent) and `o.shape` (radial default | ring | fountain). ring = flat even shockwave (angle k/n*2pi + jitter, near-constant speed, tiny out-of-plane pop); fountain = upward plume (strong +y, moderate xz) for pickups; radial = original isotropic). src/main.js (13 burst call-sites now pass `palette: CONFIG.fx.palette.X`; `shape:'ring'` for bossRing/bossDeath/shieldPop/bomb, `shape:'fountain'` for the overdrive powerup; muzzle flashes bossFire/mineFire use palette.mine; game-over confetti intentionally left single-color - bounded scope). Browser (1280x720, __vr.particles): palette - a burst with [red,green,blue,yellow] yields all 4 colors present in the color buffer (paletteSeen {r:1,g:1,b:1,y:1}). Shapes - read off the world-Y buffer (the burst plane is world (x, 0.4, y), so world-Y is the out-of-plane pop axis): after drain + 3 sync steps, radial straddles the 0.4 plane and is widest (0.357-0.440), ring is the tightest around 0.4 (0.387-0.413), fountain rises fully above it (0.686-0.923); assertions fountain.min (0.686) > radial.max (0.440), radial straddles 0.4, ring tightest - all three cleanly distinct. Screenshot .evidence/i86-particle-palettes-shapes.png (three colored clusters - orange-red ring, green-cyan fountain, yellow-magenta radial; render healthy, no NaN). Console 0 errors/0 warnings.

## I87 - sound-level pass: true-peak limiter + tiered cue weights (ST-087)

- Goal: make the SFX mix well-balanced and clip-free relative to the visual impact - no isolated cue or worst-case stacking should push the output past the device ceiling (I84 measured an isolated bomb reaching ~1.02 post-makeup, i.e. right at the clip point), and the cues should carry a deliberate dynamic range so the loudest moments read louder without anything distorting.
- Changed: src/render/audio.js (master chain now ends in a true-peak limiter after the makeup gain - a hard DynamicsCompressor (threshold -8 dB, knee 3, ratio 20, attack 0.001, release 0.06) wired before the meter + destination so the device always hears the limited signal; the CUES gain table is organized into three documented weight tiers (background: shoot/swish/mineFire; event: kill/mine/powerup/shields/boss rings/overdrive/vortex; impact: hit/bossDeath/bomb) and the two loudest impact cues are trimmed for headroom (bomb 0.7->0.55, bossDeath 0.5->0.45) so the limiter tames only the very peak rather than pumping on every big hit; new weights() probe returns the per-cue configured gains). No core/logic changes.
- Verification: node --check clean; 232/232 (render-only, count unchanged); build exit 0. Browser (gesture-initiated AudioContext, state running, meter() post-limiter): isolated bomb peak 0.7739 (was ~1.02 pre-limiter per I84) and worst-case stack (bomb+bossDeath+hit) peak 0.7969 - both comfortably under the 0.9 ceiling, with the stack pinned near the isolated-bomb peak (the limiter holding the ceiling as designed; first pass at a softer limiter measured 0.9191 and was hardened); impact stays loud (bomb 0.77 >= 0.7) while the background tier (shoot) sits at 0.1919, a clean ~4x impact-to-background dynamic range (kill event tier 0.6643). Console 0 errors/0 warnings. No rendering change (audio-only), so no screenshot.

## I88 - UI motion pass: eased screen entrance, score-popup pop, wave-banner slide (ST-088)

- Goal: make the UI motion feel deliberate and smooth - screens settle in instead of popping, score popups ease up with a punch-in scale and a fade that lingers then drops, and the wave banner eases in/out with a slide-up into place. All new movement is gated by the reduced-motion setting (the non-movement opacity fades still apply, since reduced-motion is about movement, not appearance).
- Changed: src/render/screens.js (NEW vrPanelIn keyframe - opacity 0->1 + scale 0.98->1 - applied to all three panels via the new .vr-panel class; the animation replays each time a screen goes display:none->flex so menu/pause/game-over all settle in; gated by .vr-reduced .vr-panel { animation:none }). src/render/scorePopups.js (rise now easeOutCubic - fast start, settle - instead of linear; added a start "pop" scale 0.5->1 over the first 30% (easeOutCubic) baked into the transform; opacity is now a quick fade-in (first 12%), hold, then an eased pow(1.4) fade-out; under reduced motion the rise and scale are disabled (both are movement) while the opacity fade still applies). src/render/waveBanner.js (the fade envelope is now eased - easeOutCubic in over 0.25s, easeInCubic out over the final 0.6s - instead of linear; added an entry slide-up translateY 12px->0 over 0.4s (easeOutCubic) that resets to 0 on completion; under reduced motion the slide is disabled and the existing hard 1/0 opacity behavior is kept). No core/logic changes.
- Verification: node --check clean (3 files); 232/232 (render-only, count unchanged); build exit 0. ## I104 - three.js GLB integration: fire-and-forget loader + renderer swaps (ST-104)

- Goal: wire the five Blender-pass GLBs (assets/) into the three.js render layer with fire-and-forget async loading + procedural fallback, per the integration principle in design/asset-spec.md (GLB supplies geometry; all dynamic material behavior stays in code; src/core collision untouched).
- Changed: src/render/assetLoader.js (new: GLTFLoader-backed loader with per-asset onAsset callbacks, ready flags, warn + procedural fallback on fetch failure, relative assets/ URLs that work from both dev and dist); src/render/shipMesh.js (body parts moved into a ship_body sub-group while the additive flames stay on the main group; new useGLB(obj) removes + disposes the procedural body, adds the GLB scene root, and overrides materials by name to guarantee the exact in-code palette); src/render/asteroidRenderer.js (new setGLB(size, obj) stores one GLB geometry per size; sync picks glbGeo[size] || the procedural variant; fresnel rim + golden swap + material stay in code); src/render/bossMesh.js (new useGLB(obj) swaps the geometry only, keeping the in-code pulsing-emissive material); src/main.js (wires the loader so each ready asset swaps its renderer, exposes assetLoader on window.__vr for debug); scripts/build.mjs (vendors jsm/loaders/GLTFLoader.js + jsm/utils/BufferGeometryUtils.js and copies assets/ into dist/).
- Verification: npm test 233/233 green (core untouched); npm run build exit 0 with dist/assets/*.glb present and the vendored GLTFLoader chain shipped; browser smoke on dev :5173 - all five assets ready=true, ship group body child swapped (procedural ship_body -> GLB scene root named 'Scene'; direct child count stays 3: body + two flames, body 540 verts), boss mesh geometry swapped (384 verts, bbox 4.17 x 9.0 x 4.35, origin-centered), forced L/M/S rocks rendered with the GLB geometries (960/240/240 verts = 320/80/80 tris non-indexed flat-shaded), menu + early-PLAYING screenshots show the GLB rocks + ship with a working score popup, console 0 errors / 0 warnings, no NaN, early frame 26 draw calls / 508 tris (under the full-scene baseline).

## I119 - chain-split rocks: outward kick + generation (ST-119)

- Goal: ST-119 acceptance is 'Chain-splitter rocks (core): L splits 2x M, M splits 2x S | unit: split chain + cap | node --test'.
- Record correction (grepped before building): the chain split itself is ALREADY fully implemented and tested - AsteroidField.destroy() (src/core/asteroids.js) spawns spec.splits[0..1] children of spec.child (config: L -> [2,3] M, M -> [2,3] S, S -> none), honors maxConcurrent, and tests/asteroids.test.js already covers the split rules, speedMult inheritance and the cap (ST-009). No new split mechanics were needed.
- Genuine gap filled: fragments previously spawned with a FULLY RANDOM direction (spawn(at) had ang = rng.next()*2PI) and carried no lineage. ST-119 adds the two missing chain-split behaviors: (1) outward kick - split fragments inherit the parent's heading and are biased within +/- cfg.asteroid.split.spread rad (classic asteroids momentum feel); (2) generation tracking - each rock carries gen (rim spawn 0, each split +1: L0 -> M1 -> S2), which the upcoming ST-120 split scoring will use.
- Changed: src/core/asteroids.js (spawn() gains optional dirHint + gen params - dirHint biases the 'at' spawn angle around atan2(dirHint); rock object gains gen; destroy() passes v2Norm(rock.vel) as dirHint and gen+1 to children; the boss-split call site (game.js spawn('M',1,at)) is unchanged - no dirHint keeps its legacy fully-random spread); src/core/config.js (asteroid.split.spread = 0.9 rad, data-driven); tests/asteroids.test.js (+3: generation depth L0->M1->S2, spread bound + seeded mean-direction bias with parent heading +x, split fragments capped at free slots when near maxConcurrent).
- Verified: 260/260 (was 257, +3), build exit 0, sequence 1..119. Core-only (no render/DOM change -> no browser smoke per quality bar). Determinism preserved: all new randomness flows through the seeded RNG (rng.range for the spread), same call count pattern as before on the split path.
- Next: I120 ST-120 split tuning + scoring (unit + browser) - wire gen-aware score deltas (e.g., deeper fragments worth more) in scoreKeeper or the kill event, then verify the chain read in the browser.

## I120 - split tuning + scoring: per-generation fragment bonus (ST-120)

- Goal: ST-120 acceptance is 'Splitter tuning + scoring (unit + browser)' - make the split chain readable in score: deeper fragments (produced by splitting) are worth more, so a player who keeps breaking a rock down earns an escalating payoff per kill.
- Record correction (grepped before building): gen tracking already landed in ST-119 (I119) - every rock carries gen (rim 0, L0->M1->S2) and World.resolveBulletRocks() already reads rock.size's base score + the golden multiplier. No new gen plumbing was needed; the genuine gap was that the score ignored gen entirely, so a size-2 S was worth the same whether it was a fresh rim spawn or the last fragment of a chain.
- Changed: src/core/config.js - asteroid.split gains genBonus:[0,0.25,0.5] (per-generation score bonus: gen0 +0%, gen1 +25%, gen2 +50%; the table clamps for gen>2). src/core/game.js - resolveBulletRocks() computes genBonus = genBonus[Math.min(rock.gen||0, len-1)]||0 and folds (1+genBonus) into base before scoreKeeper.kill; the kill event now also carries gen for the HUD/debug overlay.
- Bug fixed in-session: the first version indexed genBonus[rock.gen||0]||0, which returned 0 for gen beyond the 3-entry table (no clamp) - replaced with a Math.min clamp to the last table entry so any deep fragment still scores the top bonus.
- Verified (unit): 265/265 (was 260, +5) in tests/splitScoring.test.js - genBonus config sanity (non-decreasing from 0); gen-0 L scores base 25; gen-1 M scores floor(50*1.25)=62 and gen-2 S scores floor(100*1.5)=150 (fresh worlds, forced r.gen); gen 99 clamps to the last bonus; full chain L0->M1 with w.scoreKeeper.onDamage() between kills (isolates the multiplier from near-miss bonuses) + kill event carries gen.
- Verified (browser, dev :5173, ?nocache=120): killed L gen0 -> +25; M gen1 -> +62; S gen2 -> +150; final score 312 = 237 (kills) + 75 (5 near-miss * 15, pre-existing ST-053, fired because the idle ship was grazed by wave rocks) - exact decomposition confirmed via window.__vr.eventTally = {kill:3, wave:1, nearmiss:5, hit:2, powerupDrop:1}. ## I121 - shielded rocks: armored, never-split rim variant (ST-121)

- Goal: ST-121 acceptance is 'Shielded rocks (core: armor N hits, visual state, never splits | unit: armor + no-split | node --test)'.
- Record correction (grepped before building): rocks were 1-hit (no armor field) and only the Monolith boss had HP (boss.js takeHit: hp -= 1). No armor concept existed on rocks - the shielded rock is genuinely new. (A separate SHIP shield power-up already exists - powerups.isShielding()/consumeShieldHit(), event 'shieldPop' - so the rock shield uses distinct names: rock.shielded / rock.armor, AsteroidField.hit(), event 'rockShieldHit'.)
- Design: a shielded rock is a rare rim-spawn variant (like the golden rock) that absorbs armor bullet hits before shattering, NEVER splits, and pays a higher flat score. Its render shell is a later iteration (this is core-only).
- Changed: (1) src/core/config.js - asteroid.shielded { minWave: 4, chance: 0.10, armor: 3, size: 'M', score: 400 } (data-driven; reward exceeds any single rock). (2) src/core/asteroids.js - spawn() gains a shielded param (sets rock.shielded + rock.armor); destroy() gains a no-split guard (spec.splits.length && !rock.shielded) so the ship-ram and bomb paths also never split a shielded rock; new hit(id) - an armor-aware single hit (a shielded rock absorbs armor-1 until 0 then shatters via destroy; a normal rock destroys on the first hit exactly as before) returning { destroyed, shielded, armorLeft, rock, children }. (3) src/core/game.js - resolveBulletRocks() routes every rock hit through field.hit(): on an absorbed hit it emits 'rockShieldHit' (no score/combo/split) and continues; on a shatter it scores shielded.score and the kill event carries shielded. bombBlast() scores a shielded rock at shielded.score (not the size score) for consistency. (4) src/core/waves.js - the rim-spawn roll adds a shielded chance (wave >= minWave) taking precedence over golden on the same roll; shielded rocks spawn at shielded.size and are never fragments.
- Verified: 273/273 (was 265, +8) in tests/shielded.test.js - config sanity; spawn sets flag+armor (a normal rock does not); a hit below exhaustion is absorbed (no destroy/split); armor exhaustion shatters with ZERO fragments; contrast: a normal M rock at the same spot DOES split; destroy() (the ship/bomb path) never splits a shielded rock; absorbed hits score 0 + emit rockShieldHit with the correct armorLeft and the shatter pays shielded.score (kill event flags shielded); bomb shatter pays shielded.score.
- Core-only (no render/DOM change -> no browser smoke per quality bar). Determinism preserved: the new rng.chance roll lives in waves.js (seeded); all other new behavior is deterministic from config.
- Next: I122 ST-122 weapon progression lvl 1-3 (core: levels by wave, damage/rate | unit: level thresholds | node --test).

## I122 - weapon progression: permanent levels 1-3 by wave (ST-122)

- Goal: ST-122 acceptance is 'Weapon progression lvl 1-3 (core: levels by wave, damage/rate | unit: level thresholds | node --test)' - the gun should get meaningfully stronger as the run progresses, independent of the temporary power-ups.
- Record correction (grepped before building): the weapon already had a fixed fire cadence (config weapon.fireInterval) plus TWO temporary power-ups (rapid = fire-rate x2, spread = 3-way burst, both timed). There was no PERMANENT wave-driven weapon level - so levels are genuinely new. (Because rocks are 1-hit, 'damage' is expressed as bullet count + cadence, not per-bullet HP.)
- Design: a permanent weapon level 1->2->3 unlocked by wave. L1 (base, wave 1) = single shot; L2 (wave 5) = narrow twin; L3 (wave 10) = triple + faster. The spread POWER-UP overrides the level layout (count/arc) for its duration; rapid and the level rateMul STACK on cadence. Distinct from I121: this is the ship's gun, not the rock shield.
- Changed: (1) src/core/config.js - weapon.levels array [{minWave:1,count:1,arc:0,rateMul:1.0},{minWave:5,count:2,arc:0.16,rateMul:1.0},{minWave:10,count:3,arc:0.34,rateMul:1.15}] (data-driven). (2) src/core/game.js - new weaponLevel() (1-based, highest level whose minWave <= wave) + weaponSpec() getters; constructor + reset track this._wlevel; step() emits 'weaponLevelUp' {level} when the level rises as the wave advances; fireWeapon() now reads the level spec for count/arc and multiplies the level rateMul onto the rapid multiplier, and the 'fire' event carries level (for the I123 render pass).
- Verified: 279/279 (was 273, +6) in tests/weaponLevel.test.js - config levels sane + minWave strictly increasing; thresholds map wave->level (0/1/4->1, 5/9->2, 10/99->3); weaponSpec reflects the level; fireWeapon fires the level bullet count (L1 -> 1, L3 -> 3); crossing a threshold emits weaponLevelUp and tracks _wlevel; the fire event carries level.
- Core-only (no render/DOM change -> no browser smoke per quality bar). Determinism preserved: the level is a pure function of the (deterministic) wave; no new randomness.
- Next: I123 ST-123 weapon visuals per level (browser) - render the twin/triple layout + a level-up flash/HUD cue.

## I123 - weapon visuals per level: bullet tint + WPN pips + level-up toast (ST-123)

- Goal: ST-123 acceptance is 'Weapon visuals: bullet color/trail per level' (browser) - make the permanent weapon level from I122 visible and readable in the render.
- Record correction (grepped before building): the core already fired the per-level bullet count (L1=1/L2=2/L3=3 via fireWeapon + weaponSpec, I122) and emitted 'weaponLevelUp' {level}; bullets already auto-rendered via the shared-material bulletPool. So the genuine new render work = per-level BULLET COLOR / TRAIL TINT + a HUD weapon-level indicator + a level-up toast/sfx (no new core logic, no new unit tests expected - render-only).
- Design: (1) config fx.palette.bulletLevel = [0xbff4ff, 0x8affc0, 0xffd27a] (L1 cool-cyan / L2 mint / L3 amber). (2) bulletPool.setLevel(level) recolors the SINGLE shared bullet head material + the additive trail tint from that palette entry (no-op on repeat via a curLevel cache) - deliberately NO per-bullet materials, keeping the one-material low-draw-call rule (stragglers mid-level-change are an accepted minor inaccuracy). (3) HUD gains a WPN indicator (top-left under the score): 'WPN' label + 3 pips that fill by level and recolor to the level color; written only on change (cache['wpn']). (4) main.js: SFX_CUES weaponLevelUp -> 'levelUp'; the event dispatch toasts 'WEAPON LEVEL n' (reuses the aria-live toast); syncVisuals calls bullets.setLevel(world.weaponLevel()). (5) audio levelUp cue: a bright rising 3-note arpeggio (C5-E5-G5, event tier).
- Changed: src/core/config.js (palette.bulletLevel), src/render/bulletPool.js (setLevel + curLevel cache, exported), src/render/hud.js (hexColor helper + WPN box/pips + render pip update), src/main.js (SFX_CUES + weaponLevelUp toast + bullets.setLevel in syncVisuals), src/render/audio.js (levelUp cue).

L3 (wave 10): weaponLevel 3, 3 bullets/shot, head + trail #ffd27a (amber), pips all amber, toast 'WEAPON LEVEL 3'. console 0/0, 0 NaN, draw calls low (27-38), tris ~856. ## I124 - balance pass E8-1: audit + bomb/genBonus parity fix (ST-124)

- Goal: ST-124 acceptance is 'Balance pass E8-1 (new-system deltas) | notes + config | doc + node' - audit the score/fire-rate deltas of the E8B systems (weapon levels, shielded rocks, split gen bonus, drones) and tune in config where warranted.
- Record correction (evidence before tuning): pulled the full economy from config.js + the scoring path in game.js. The E8B systems are well-balanced - no exploitable inflation. DPS tracks the difficulty ramp (5.5 -> 11 -> ~19 bullets/s vs rock count 14 -> 24 -> cap 36 + speed cap 2.1x); per-kill value is fixed (so higher DPS = more throughput on harder waves = intended progression, not a farm); near-miss is per-approach (not fire-spammable); golden + genBonus are mutually exclusive in practice (golden is rim-only and never a fragment); combo cap 2.4x + overdrive x2 bound the per-kill ceiling (max all-stacked: shielded 1920 / boss 4800).
- Bounded lever (the one genuine gap found): the bullet-kill path applied the split per-gen bonus (gen1 +25% / gen2 +50%) but the BOMB path did not, so a gen-2 S cleared by bomb under-scored (100 vs 150). Fixed: game.js bombBlast now applies the same (1 + genBonus) multiplier (still a flat 400 for shielded, which never splits). One expression, no new tunable.
- Changed: src/core/game.js (bombBlast genBonus parity), tests/bombGenBonus.test.js (new, 4 tests), design/balance-e8b.md (new - the full audit: scoring table, DPS vs wave ramp, balance lever, exploit risk, pacing, telemetry, risks, verification).
- Verified: 283/283 (was 279, +4) - bomb gen-1 M = 62, bomb gen-2 S = 150, bomb/bullet parity for a gen-2 S, bomb on a gen-1 shielded rock = flat 400. build 0. Doc + node (no render change -> no browser smoke per quality bar).
- Decision: no other E8-1 config change is warranted (DPS, values, and pacing are all within range); the balance stays as tuned.
- Next: I125 ST-125 determinism re-verify (node) - replay bit-identical with the new systems + this fix.

## I134 - Boss radial bolt burst: new Monolith attack pattern (ST-134)

- Story: ST-134 | Goal: "Boss radial burst pattern (core) | unit: burst geometry | node".
- Change: config.boss.burst {every 4.0, count 12, speed 7.0, offsetSpin 0.45, everyMult 0.65}; pure exported bossBurstVectors(cfg, offset) in boss.js (12 directions evenly on the circle, rotated by offset, pre-scaled to speed - no rng, so it does not perturb the shared stream); World.bossBurst() fires the volley through the existing enemyBullets pool (cap 24 may trim a volley) and emits 'bossBurst' {pos, fired}; bossBurstTimer (slow-mo scaled like the other boss timers) + bossBursts volley counter (spiral offset) initialized/reset at constructor + spawnBossForWave + the reset path with the other boss state.
- Design rationale: the boss already has the ring (destroyable rocks) and the aimed bolt; the burst is a third pressure axis - fast, undestroyable, radial, dodge-only, with per-volley spiral rotation so successive volleys do not line up. Speed 7.0 sits below the aimed bolt's 9 to keep reaction time; the enraged x0.65 cadence stacks on ST-133 phase 2.

## I138 - Asteroid belt: drifting indestructibles (core) (ST-138)

- Design: from wave 8, each wave start adds one DRIFTING belt rock at the arena rim (kept 10 u from the ship) - a slow ambient hazard (0.5-1.1 u/s, random heading) that persists for the rest of the run, up to 5 concurrent (config.belt). Belt rocks are INDESTRUCTIBLE: bullets spark off them (bullet consumed, no score/split, beltHit event), bombs ignore them, ship contact costs a life but the rock survives (normal shield/invuln rules). Belt rocks sit outside the normal rock cap (separate persistent layer) and can still award near-miss points. GDD section 'Asteroid Belt (ST-138)' added (after the WARDEN section).
- Core: asteroids.js - spawn() gains a belt flag (9th arg): belt rocks are exempt from the maxConcurrent cap and carry belt: true; hit() returns {destroyed:false, belt:true, rock} for belt rocks; shatter() refuses belt rocks (bomb-proof); destroy() never splits belt rocks (guard beside shielded). game.js - wave start calls spawnBeltForWave(wv.startedWave) after the warden hook; spawnBeltForWave(wave) (wave >= fromWave, count < max): rim spawn 10 u from the ship through the field, then a random-heading drift velocity drawn from cfg.belt.speed; resolveBulletRocks emits 'beltHit' {pos} and continues (no score/split) when field.hit reports belt; resolveShipRocks skips field.destroy for belt rocks (they survive contact, the ship still takes the normal shield/damage path).
- Config: config.belt {fromWave 8, max 5, size 'M', speed [0.5, 1.1]}.
- Events: beltHit {pos} (FX in I139: white-ish spark, subtle).
- Tests: 389/389 (was 378, +11 in tests/belt.test.js: config shape; spawn rules (nothing before wave 8, one per wave, capped at 5); rim spawn kept 10 u from a rim-parked ship + drift speed in range; cap exemption (belt spawns with the field at maxConcurrent); field.hit indestructibility (20 hits, never destroyed); live-bullet path (bullet consumed, rock alive, no fragments, no score, no kill event, beltHit with exact pos); bomb shatter refused; destroy() no fragments; ship contact (1 life lost, rock survives, invuln blocks a second hit); 60 s drift containment; determinism (same seed -> identical pos/vel)).
- Build: 0.
- Playtest sanity: node scripts/playtest.mjs scenario 15 ran clean (belt rocks appear from wave 8; the bot takes belt contact hits as designed - a hazard, not a crash).
- Result: DONE. Next I139 ST-139 belt tuning + perf (browser): belt rock visual distinction in rockRenders, beltHit spark FX, perf check (5 belt + 24 normal rocks), playtest A/B.

## I140 - Daily seeded run (core) (ST-140)

- Design: a DAILY run is one canonical seeded run per calendar day. The seed is derived purely from the date (FNV-1a 32-bit over 'salt:YYYY-MM-DD'), so every player who starts the daily on the same day gets an identical run - a fair, comparable daily leaderboard. The seed is just a 32-bit int fed to the existing RNG/World; no core gameplay logic changes. The menu button + DAILY badge are I141 (browser).
- Core: src/core/daily.js (new, DOM-free) - fnv1a(str) -> unsigned 32-bit; dailyKey(y,m,d) -> zero-padded 'YYYY-MM-DD'; dailySeed(y,m,d,salt='daily') -> fnv1a(salt + ':' + key); dailyRng(y,m,d,salt) -> new RNG(dailySeed(...)); dailyForDate(date,salt) -> {key,seed} for the local calendar day (1-based month; UI path, not the deterministic test path).
- Config: config.daily {salt 'daily'} - namespaces the daily seed family (future-proofing; tests reference CONFIG.daily.salt).
- Tests: 399/399 (was 389, +10 in tests/daily.test.js: config shape; fnv1a unsigned+deterministic+input-sensitive; dailyKey zero-padding; dailySeed 32-bit range; determinism (5 repeated calls); 7 spread dates -> 7 distinct seeds; salt namespacing (different salt differs, config salt == default); dailyForDate 1-based-month parity with the pure path; identical RNG stream from the same day; bit-identical 400-step World run (rock pos/vel snapshots at t=1s/3s/6.6s) from the same daily seed).
- Build: 0. (Core-only - no rendering changed, no browser smoke.)
- Result: DONE. Next I141 ST-141 daily UI (browser): DAILY button in the menu seeds the run from today + a DAILY badge on the score screen.

## I141 - Daily UI: menu button, today seed, daily best, DAILY badge (browser) (ST-141)

- Design: wire the ST-140 daily seed into the UI. A DAILY RUN button in the menu starts a run seeded from today's canonical seed (dailyForDate), so every player today plays the identical board (comparable daily leaderboard). While a daily run is active a DAILY badge shows on the game-over screen, and the menu shows today's daily best (rolls over each calendar day). Restarting a daily run re-seeds to the SAME stored seed so the run reproduces exactly (it stays today's board for the session even across a midnight rollover, until you quit to menu and start DAILY again).
- Core: src/core/game.js - World.reseed(seed): resets the shared RNG (this.rng.reset(seed)) so a run can start from a specific seed; returns this (chainable). All rng-using subsystems share the one RNG instance, so a single reseed deterministically re-seeds the whole run. reset() consumes no RNG and waves.js derives its per-wave RNG from this.rng.seed, so reseed -> begin()/restart() is fully deterministic from the seed.
- Storage: src/core/storage.js - new dailyBestKey ('voidrunner.daily'); getDailyBest() -> {key, value} | null; recordDaily(key, score) raises today's daily best (or rolls the day over when the key differs), ignores zero scores, and returns {newBest, value}. Versioned + corrupt-safe like the rest of the store.
- Main: src/main.js - imports dailyForDate; new daily state (dailyMode, dailyKey, dailySeedVal, dailyNewBest, dailyMenu) + startRun(mode)/startDaily()/restartRun() helpers that reseed when daily; the screens actions and the Enter/R keyboard paths all route through them (Enter in MENU = normal run, the DAILY button = daily run, Enter/R restart re-seed when daily). On a daily game over it calls storage.recordDaily and flags dailyNewBest. screenOpts carries dailyMode/dailyKey/dailyMenu/dailyNewBest; the menu's daily line refreshes on day rollover / after a daily run.
- UI: src/render/screens.js - a DAILY RUN button in the menu (keyboard + touch, aria-labelled) calling actions.startDaily; a menu daily line ('DAILY <key>  -  BEST <n>' / 'NO BEST YET'); a pill DAILY RUN badge on the game-over screen (shows the daily key + 'NEW DAILY BEST' when today's best was raised).
- Tests: 405/405 (was 399, +6: tests/daily.test.js reseed returns-this and reseed makes the run independent of the pre-reseed RNG (two Worlds with very different initial seeds, both reseeded to the same daily seed, produce bit-identical runs); tests/storage.test.js daily best records/raises, rolls over to a new day, ignores zero scores, persists across instances, and survives a corrupt payload).
- Build: 0.
- Browser smoke: dev :5173 ?nocache=141 - menu shows the DAILY RUN button + 'DAILY 2026-11-10  -  NO BEST YET'; clicking DAILY -> state PLAYING and world.rng.seed == today's daily seed (FNV-1a('daily:2026-11-10') = 317547475) -> reseed confirmed; forced GAME_OVER -> the 'DAILY RUN  -  2026-11-10' badge renders; 0 NaN, console 0/0. Screenshots: .evidence/I141/{menu,gameover}.png.
- Result: DONE. Next I142 ST-142 shareable result string (score+seed+wave, encode/decode, unit: codec round-trip).

## I142 - Shareable result codec (core) (ST-142)

- Design: a compact, URL-safe string that encodes a run result (score, wave, seed, kills) so a player can share a result and others can replay the identical board (the seed) and see the score. The codec is pure core (DOM-free) so it is unit-testable; the UI surface (copy-to-clipboard on the game-over screen) is a later E8D iteration.
- Core: src/core/share.js (new, DOM-free) - encodeResult(result, magic?) -> 'vr1.<b36 seed>.<b36 score>.<b36 wave>.<b36 kills>.<2-char ck>' where ck = (fnv1a(body) % 1296).toString(36).padStart(2,'0') (reuses daily.js fnv1a); decodeResult(str, magic?) -> {ok:true,seed,score,wave,kills} | {ok:false}. Validation: must be a string, exactly 6 dot-parts, matching magic, base36+dot charset, valid checksum, and sane ranges (seed <= 0xffffffff, score >= 0, wave >= 1, kills >= 0).
- Config: config.share {magic 'vr1'} - namespacing/versioning the codec family.
- Tests: 415/415 (was 405, +10 in tests/share.test.js: config shape; round-trip; determinism; different-input-differs; compact/URL-safe (6 parts, magic, 2-char ck, base36 charset); rejects invalid (null/number/empty/garbage/wrong-magic/corrupted-checksum); rejects uppercase+space; edge zero; edge large (32-bit seed, big score); wrong-magic).
- Build: 0. (Core-only - no rendering changed, no browser smoke.)
- Result: DONE. Next I143 ST-143 boss variant 2 [CUTTABLE] (core+render).

## I143 - Boss variant 2: the Splicer (core+render) (ST-143)

- Design: the second boss kind, alternating every boss spawn (Monolith, Splicer, Monolith, ...). The Splicer is a different fight from the Monolith: it glides from the rim to a hold radius (11 u) and orbits slowly around the arena center, and instead of rock rings / the radial burst (Monolith only) it emits a continuous two-arm spiral of undestroyable bolts - a 'spinning sun' the ship weaves through - plus a slow aimed bolt. One-way enrage (phase 2) below the HP threshold. This is the [CUTTABLE] story: the Splicer is the first boss variant in its own class file.
- Core: src/core/splicer.js (new, DOM-free) - class Splicer: entrance glide (spawnR -> holdRadius over enterTime, no teleport; bolts gated on entered), slow orbit (orbitSpeed, random orbit direction), two-arm spiral (arms, armSpin, spiralEvery) queuing unit-direction bolts in _pending, drainBolts() -> [{x,y}] | null (World fires them into the enemyBullets pool at boltSpeed), 1 damage per hit (hit() returns true on kill), phase 2 enrage below phase2.hpFrac (faster orbit/arm spin/spin, tighter spiral, shorter aim cadence). splicerHpForWave(cfg, wave) mirrors the Monolith's endless-arc HP curve scaled over the Splicer's own base (30) and cap (72).
- Core (game.js): World ctor+reset bossSpawnCount=0; spawnBossForWave(wave) alternates by bossSpawnCount%2 (0=Monolith, 1=Splicer), uses the per-variant radius for the rim spawn point, emits {kind}; step() drains the Splicer's drainBolts() -> bossSpiralBolts() and gates the radial burst to Monolith only; new World.bossSpiralBolts(bolts) (world fires queued bolts at boltSpeed) and World.bossAimEvery() (slow aimed-bolt cadence, enrage-scaled); bossDeath reads the per-variant score/splitCount/splitSize and emits {kind}. resolveBulletBoss is unchanged (both kinds use hit()).
- Core (boss.js): Monolith ctor gains kind='monolith', score=b.score, splitCount, splitSize so both bosses share one contract (pos, radius, hp/maxHp, alive, hpFrac, entered, phase, kind, score, splitCount, splitSize, step, hit).
- Config: config.splicer {maxHp 30, hpFirstWave 15, hpStep 8, hpCap 72, radius 2.6, score 600, splitCount 2, splitSize 'M', holdRadius 11, orbitSpeed 0.5, spin 0.4, enterTime 1.2, arms 2, armSpin 1.2, spiralEvery 0.26, boltSpeed 7.0, aimEvery 2.6, phase2 {hpFrac 0.5, orbitSpeedMult 1.4, armSpinMult 1.5, spiralEveryMult 0.6, aimEveryMult 0.7, spin 1.2}}. Balance note for I144: the spiral queues ~7.7 bolts/s and with 4 s bolt life the steady state exceeds the enemyBullets cap (24), so fire() trims; I144 tunes spiralEvery/boltSpeed so the ship can reliably thread gaps.

## I144 - Balance pass E8-2: the boss family, Splicer spiral threadability (cgs-balance-check) (ST-144)

- Design: audited the E8-2 heavyweight family (Monolith, Warden, Splicer) as one system: HP curves vs the weapon ramp, the score ladder, enemyBullets pool pressure, and the Splicer's spiral dodgeability. One finding: the Splicer's phase-1 spiral was effectively unthreadable.
- Finding: the linear gap between consecutive bolts along an arm is boltSpeed x spiralEvery = 7 x 0.26 = 1.82u, while the ship's effective obstacle width is ship diameter + 2 x bolt radius = 1.8 + 0.44 = 2.24u. Gap < obstacle = a wall that cannot be crossed at any fixed angle (zero/negative margin). On top of that the phase-1 rate (7.7 bolts/s x 4s life = 30.8) exceeded the 24-bolt pool cap, so fire() silently dropped ~22% of the spiral - a broken pattern, not a dense one. Browser-confirmed: an idle ship standing at the arena center took 3 hits in ~10 s.
- Balance Lever (2 values, no new tunable): splicer.spiralEvery 0.26 -> 0.6 (phase-1 gap 4.2u = 1.88x the obstacle - threadable; rate 3.3/s) and splicer.phase2.spiralEveryMult 0.6 -> 0.65 (enrage gap 2.73u = 1.22x the obstacle - tight but fair; rate 5.1/s). The 'spinning sun' read survives (the arm still turns 41 deg p1 / 37 deg p2 per shot). Monolith + Warden numbers unchanged (already tuned I133/I134/I136; in range on this audit).
- Tests: 428/428 (was 426, +2 tests/splicer.test.js balance invariants: gap-vs-obstacle both phases (p1 >= 1.8x and p2 >= 1.1x of the 2.24u obstacle), and worst-case steady-state pool occupancy under the 24 cap both phases (p1 14.9, p2 22.7). These lock the contract so a later tune cannot silently re-close the wall.)
- Design doc: design/balance-e8-2.md (boss family table, pool pressure table, exploit risk, pacing, telemetry, risks) per the cgs-balance-check output contract.
- Build: 0.
- Browser smoke: dev :5173 ?nocache=144 - forced Splicer; phase-1 live-bolt steady state 9-12 (cap 24, no drops); forced enrage (hp 30 -> 14 via hit(), verified applied, phase 2) - live bolts 15-20, still under cap; 0 NaN, console 0/0. Screenshot: .evidence/I144/I144_spiral_gap.png (threadable bolt spacing, SPLICER bar 60%).
- Result: DONE. Next I145 ST-145 test batch E8C (25+ new tests; the two balance invariants are the template).

## I145 - Test batch E8C: 28 cross-system invariant tests (ST-145)

- Scope: new tests/e8cBatch.test.js locks the E8-2 cross-system contracts as one batch (boss alternation, heavyweight exclusivity, the HP/score family, enemyBullets pool pressure, belt coexistence, determinism, edge cases) - 28 tests in 5 sections, using the I144 balance invariants (gap-vs-obstacle, pool occupancy) as the template for what a cross-system test looks like.
- Sections: alternation (a skip does not advance bossSpawnCount; variant order preserved through skips; bossSpawn event kind; a live boss of either kind blocks the Warden; empty slot spawns the Warden; one Warden at a time; rim spawn stays inside the arena for seeds 1-10 with the ship near the rim; the tries loop terminates with the ship ON the ring) | HP/score family (per-variant HP at every 5-wave spawn via spawnBossForWave; Splicer <= Monolith at all shared waves; hpFirstWave boundary = base HP; score ladder warden < splicer < monolith) | pool pressure (60 s sustained Splicer phase 2 never exceeds the 24 cap; fire() returns null exactly at the cap and a cull frees a slot; a bolt passes THROUGH a belt rock - no bolt-vs-rock collision exists; 5 belt rocks + the spiral coexist; a Warden spawns on a belt-active wave 10) | determinism (two seeded Worlds with the same Monolith -> Splicer script are bit-identical via snapshot(); a skip + respawn script is bit-identical - the skip consumes no RNG; a daily-seeded run through both bosses is bit-identical) | edge cases (kill before the 5th wave -> next variant exactly once; no aimed bolt before aimEvery elapses; spiral angle coverage > 5 rad over 10 shots; the family ctor hp contract - optional, positive-clamped, config fallback - for all three heavies; a skipped wave touches neither the Warden slot nor the belt layer).
- Fix while authoring: the "bolt passes through a belt rock" test carried an inverted distance assertion (the bolt starts 2 u inside the rock's spawn point and ends 2.48 u PAST it, so dist(bolt, rock) < dist(from, rock) can never hold). Rewrote it as a real contract check: the bolt survives all 40 steps, travels the full 7 u/s x 0.64 s distance with unchanged velocity, and sits on the far side of the rock; the belt rock is alive and still at its spawn position. Same strength, no longer always-false.
- Tests: 456/456 (was 428, +28).
- Build: 0.
- Browser smoke: not applicable (tests-only, no rendering changes).

## I151 - First-run tutorial overlay: dismiss + persist (ST-151)

- Story: ST-151 | Goal: a one-time, richer, dismissible "how to play" card shown on the player's first entry to PLAYING (distinct from the passive 6s tutorialHint); gate the sim while visible so they read without dying; dismiss via button + Enter/Space/Esc; persist the dismissal so it never re-shows. Opens E8D (UI/UX).
- Design: dedicated storage key (Storage.getTutorialSeen/setTutorialSeen, versioned payload, mirrors the daily pattern; no config.js change - the constructor defaults the key to 'voidrunner.tutorial'). New src/render/tutorialOverlay.js (createTutorialOverlay, mirrors the screens.js panel/line/keycap style, reduced-motion aware via the vr-reduced class toggled from syncVisuals). startRun branches on !getTutorialSeen(): first run shows the overlay + sets the gate, otherwise shows the passive hint. tick() early-returns while tutorialActive (sim frozen, render keeps going). keydown guard owns Enter/Space/Esc/p/P while active (preventDefault + dismiss); togglePause no-ops while active so touch two-finger pause cannot slip in.
- Changed: src/core/storage.js (tutorialKey + getTutorialSeen/setTutorialSeen), src/render/tutorialOverlay.js (new), src/main.js (import, overlay + tutorialActive + dismissTutorial after storage, startRun branch, togglePause guard, keydown guard, tick gate, tutorial.sync in syncVisuals, __vr tutorial handles), tests/storage.test.js (+3 tests), docs.

## I152 - Session stats: accuracy + sessionStats API (ST-152)

- Story: ST-152 | Goal: add the one genuinely new stat - accuracy (hits / shots fired) - and expose a clean, testable session-stats snapshot for the UI. Kills, max combo, wave and time already render at game over; accuracy is the new number.
- Design: ScoreKeeper tracks shotsFired/shotsHit: onShotFired(n) floors + clamps (ignores non-finite/negative), onShotHit(), accuracy() = min(1, hits/fired) with 0 before the first shot, reset() clears both. World.onPlayerBulletHit(bulletId) wraps weapons.markHit + scoreKeeper.onShotHit so every player-bullet hit counts in one place - all 5 raw markHit sites (rocks, mines, boss, warden, drones) now route through it; enemy bolts (fireAimedBolt / enemyBullets.fire) are deliberately NOT counted. World counts onShotFired(shots.length) at the muzzle (spread fires count n at once). World.sessionStats() returns { score, kills, wave, maxCombo, shotsFired, shotsHit, accuracy, time }. The game-over screen gains a third stats line: "ACCURACY % SHOTS h/f" (cyan, same panel style, --vr-fs scaled).
- Changed: src/core/score.js (2 fields + 3 methods + reset), src/core/game.js (sessionStats, onPlayerBulletHit, fire-site onShotFired, 5 call-site swaps: 3x b.id + 2x blt.id), src/render/screens.js (statsLine3 + set call), tests/score.test.js (+4), docs.
- Tests: 466/466 (was 462, +4: accuracy 0 before any shot; rises with hits and caps at 1; onShotFired floors/clamps/ignores bad input; reset clears counters).
- Browser: Playwright ?nocache=152b. Firing run: 77 shots, 22 hits, accuracy 0.28571... = exactly 22/77, in [0,1]; ship pos/vel finite throughout (the one NaN seen mid-smoke was a smoke-input bug - scalar thrust where advance() wants a {x,y} vector; with the vector the ship holds its position exactly). Forced death through the real resolveShipRocks kamikaze path (exactly one hit + one gameover event): the game-over screen shows "ACCURACY 29%    SHOTS 22/77" under the KILLS / MAX COMBO lines (screenshot). Deep NaN scan (151 objects): 0 real non-finite (only the intentional scoreKeeper.lastKillTime=-Infinity sentinel).
- Console: 0 errors / 0 warnings.
- Screenshot: .evidence/I152/I152_session_stats.png (game-over with the accuracy line).

## I153 - Kill feed: fading top-right list of recent kills (ST-153)

- Story: ST-153 | Goal: a compact, glanceable record of the last few kills (what + points + combo), so the player tracks what they are destroying without relying only on score popups. Pure render feature - no core logic change (driven by the existing 'kill' events).

Labels: S/M/L ROCK, GOLDEN ROCK (gold), MINE, SEEKER, WARDEN (orange, big), THE MONOLITH / THE SPLICER (gold, big, from the boss kind tag). Points always; "xN" combo (rounded to 1 dp - the 0.1 combo step produces float noise like 2.4000000000000004 without rounding) and "OD" when overdrive is active. Accessibility: aria-hidden (decorative HUD; popups + game-over stats already carry the data), reduced-motion aware (vr-reduced: no slide-in, no fade), --vr-fs font scaling, mobile media query.

## I155 - Colorblind shape cues + toggle (ST-155)

- Story: ST-155 | Goal: identity/state cues that read without color, for colorblind players. Setting shapeCues (default true) gives golden rocks a gold wireframe icosahedron cage and the Warden an 8-spike collar that extends only when exposed (armor spent), so both read by silhouette, not just hue/glow. Toggle in settings (menu + pause), persisted.
- Design (cgs-ux-design / accessibility): cage = shared IcosahedronGeometry(1,0) wireframe (MeshBasicMaterial #ffd24d, opacity 0.85, depthWrite false) parented as children[1] of each pooled rock mesh (children[0] is the shield shell); sync sets cage.visible = shapeCuesOn && r.golden && !r.belt and scale radius*1.5. Warden spikes: 8 cones in holders (rotation.z = -PI/2 so they point outward), spikes.visible = shapeCuesOn && !armored; hidden on death. Boss/mine intentionally excluded: boss already has size + health bar, mine already has a distinct sphere shape + pulse - no redundant geometry. Checkbox row "SHAPE CUES" in the VISUALS card; both panel instances (menu, pause) sync through sync().

## I156 - Colorblind verification + palette check (ST-156)

- Story: ST-156 | Goal: empirically verify (not assume) that the I155 shape cues survive real color-vision-deficiency simulation, and run a full palette check across every entity color. Verification-only iteration - no src changes.
- Method (cgs-test-evidence-review / a11y): staged a live scene containing all entity types (normal rock, golden rock, belt rock, warden armored + exposed, mine, seeker drone, enemy bolt, ship) at zero drift; captured 3 baseline frames; applied Machado 2009 CVD matrices (deuteranopia, protanopia) + standard tritanopia matrix in LINEAR light (sRGB -> linear -> matrix -> sRGB) to (a) the full rendered frames and (b) a 21-color entity palette taken from code, scoring 15 functional pairs with CIE76 dE in Lab. New reusable dev-only tool: tools/colorblind.cjs (pngjs; tools/node_modules gitignored, tools/package.json private).
- PASS criteria (verified): warden armored vs exposed dE 49-106 (distinct in all 3 sims - state reads by color AND spike silhouette); golden rock vs normal rock dE 41-66 (distinct, cage reinforces); enemy bolt vs player bullet dE 63-111 (distinct); cage gold vs ship blue dE 69-99. Visual review: cage wireframe + warden spikes read as silhouettes in all 3 sims (deuteranopia + protanopia frames inspected); cues-OFF control frame shows the golden rock degrading to a brightness-only cue - confirming the cage does the work.
- Confusable-by-color pairs (all mitigated, none blocking - documented): pu_shield vs ship_cockpit dE 0.0-0.9 (same cyan by design; torus vs cone shape; both friendly); enemy_bolt vs mine dE 4.5-9.9 (fast elongated vs pulsing sphere; both threats, motion separates); enemy_bolt vs warden_exposed dE 8.4-18.5 (size + spikes); mine vs pu_bomb dE 13-19.8 (shape + pulse + drift vs proximity context); warden_armored teal vs ship/drone cyan dE 12.5-29.8 (size 2-4x, armor-shell silhouette; under protanopia the warden reads as neutral gray - MORE distinct); golden_rock vs warden_exposed dE 21-28.3 (the worst hue pair - resolved exactly by I155 cage-vs-spikes); pu_rapid vs pu_spread dE 16-25.7 (octahedron vs cone). No palette change required.
- Changed: tools/colorblind.cjs (new - CVD frame simulation + palette dE table), tools/package.json (new - pngjs dev-only), docs. No src/ changes.

## I157 - Hotkeys: F fx toggle (M/P regression-verified) (ST-157)

- Story: ST-157 | Goal: one-press hotkeys for the three most common in-flight setting changes. P (pause) and M (mute) already existed and work in all states; F is NEW - toggles FX quality high<->low so a player can drop to low FX on a struggling machine without opening settings. All three are documented in the menu keycap grid and (M/F) on the pause screen.
- Design (cgs-ux-design): F flips fx low<->high and mirrors the settings-panel select, the toast, the bloom on/off, and persistence. User choice overrides adaptive auto-downgrade (adaptive.userSet), so an auto-low from frame-time will not silently fight a manual high. Panel-focus guard: while a settings-panel control is focused, f/F is swallowed (stopPropagation) so it cannot be an accidental toggle - consistent with the existing M guard. Menu keycaps gain an ['F','fx'] row; the pause screen gains an 'F — FX (HIGH|LOW)' row next to the existing 'M — MUTE' row, both reflecting the live value each frame.
- Changed: src/core/storage.js (export toggleFxQuality(fx): 'low' <-> 'high', non-'low' treated as 'high' to mirror applySettings; JSDoc), src/main.js (import toggleFxQuality; new keydown branch after M: getSettings -> toggleFxQuality -> setSettings -> applySettings -> adaptive.userSet(next) -> fxPreviewFn(next) -> menu/pause panel sync() -> toast('FX '+LEVEL); screenOpts.fx wired in syncVisuals), src/render/screens.js (menu keycaps ['F','fx']; pause 'F — FX' row in build + PAUSED branch; actionsRow guard list gains f/F), src/render/settingsPanel.js (keydown hotkey guard gains f/F), tests/storage.test.js (+1), docs.
- Tests: 468/468 (was 467, +1 toggleFxQuality: high->low, low->high, undefined->low, 'garbage'->low, double-flip identity).
- Browser: Playwright (?nocache=157 then 157b). Menu shows the F/fx keycap. F -> toast 'FX LOW', both panel fx selects -> LOW, liveSettings().fx 'low', bloom.enabled false, storage.getSettings().fx 'low' (persisted). F again -> 'FX HIGH', bloom re-enabled, select HIGH. Persistence: reload to ?nocache=157b with fx=LOW -> stays LOW (bloom off, select low). Pause screen shows the 'F — FX (HIGH)' row. Regression: M toggles 'M — MUTE' OFF->ON; Q quits pause->menu. Guard verified with a synthetic bubbling keydown 'f': dispatched on a panel select it does NOT change fx (guard), dispatched on document.body it toggles high->low (handler alive) then restored.
- Console: 0 errors / 0 warnings (fresh loads).
- Screenshots: .evidence/I157/I157_fx_low_toast.png (menu: F/fx keycap + FX QUALITY LOW), I157_pause_fx_row.png (pause: 'F — FX (HIGH)' row + 'M — MUTE').

## I158 - Options rows: music / colorblind preview / slow-mo hint (ST-158)

- Story: ST-158 | Goal: expose three real, persisted features as settings-panel rows so the accessibility + audio work from I155/156 is user-reachable, not just a code default.
- Design (cgs-ux-design, a11y + keyboard nav): three new rows, each a real capability (not a decorative toggle). MUSIC (AUDIO card): a generative ambient bed (soft minor pad + sparse pentatonic pluck) on its own lowpass gain bus under master, independent of the ST-083 drone ambience, driven in syncVisuals exactly like ambience (PLAYING + the music setting) with idempotent start/stop so state transitions never double-start or leak nodes. COLORBLIND (VISUALS card): a deuteranopia (Machado 2009) preview that applies an inline-SVG feColorMatrix as a CSS filter on the on-screen canvas - the same matrix tools/colorblind.cjs used in I156, applied in sRGB for a close preview - so a player can see the game the way a deuteranope does and confirm the I155 shape cues carry the identity. SLOW-MO HINT (VISUALS card): a short caption shown only while a slow-mo pickup is active or on the field (PLAYING only), teaching what the power-up does (enemies slow to 40%, you stay full speed) without reading the HUD.

## I159 - Game-over share screen: copy reproducible result code (ST-159)

- Story: ST-159 | Goal: give players a one-tap way to share a finished run. ST-142 already built the reproducible, checksummed, URL-safe result codec (encodeResult/decodeResult in src/core/share.js) but it was unwired. This iteration exposes it on the game-over screen.
- Design (cgs-ux-design, input path + a11y): a single 'COPY RESULT' button under the restart prompt. It copies the run's reproducible result code (magic + seed + score + wave + kills + FNV-1a checksum) to the clipboard - the seed makes the result verifiable/replayable (reseed the World to that seed). Primary path is navigator.clipboard.writeText; a textarea + document.execCommand('copy') fallback covers older/locked-down contexts. Feedback flashes 'COPIED!' / 'COPY FAILED' for 1.4s (button disabled during the window to prevent double-copies), then reverts to 'COPY RESULT'. The button is a native <button> (focusable, Enter/Space operable) with an aria-label, so keyboard and touch both work; the existing '#screens :focus-visible' ring applies. A small caption ('copies a reproducible result code') sets expectations.
- Changed: src/render/screens.js (import encodeResult; module-scoped goShare set each frame in the GAME_OVER update branch from world.rng.seed / world.score / wave(clamped >=1) / world.scoreKeeper.kills; COPY RESULT button + caption; copyShare()/legacyCopy() helpers with clipboard+fallback and timed feedback; return object gains lastShare() + shareButton), src/main.js (window.__vr.lastShare() probe), tests/share.test.js (+1 World->codec wiring-contract test). No new core logic (reuses the ST-142 codec).
- Tests: 470/470 (was 469, +1: a share code built from a real World's rng.seed/score/wave/kills decodes back to identical values; wave uses Math.max(1,world.wave) to mirror the production clamp - the first version compared raw world.wave (0 before the first step) against the codec's >=1 clamp and failed, confirming the clamp matters).
- Browser: Playwright (?nocache=159). Started a run (Enter, PLAYING), forced GAME_OVER via world.game.to('GAME_OVER') (a legal PLAYING->GAME_OVER transition). Game-over panel visible (display:flex); COPY RESULT button present with default text + 'copies a reproducible result code' caption. lastShare() returned 'vr1.jaa.1e.1.1.5a' (6 dot-fields, magic vr1, base36 charset) = seed 24994 / score 50 / wave 1 / kills 1 - format-valid. Click -> button text flashed 'COPIED!' and disabled=true at t+150ms (the primary navigator.clipboard.writeText resolved, not the fallback), reverting to 'COPY RESULT' after the 1.4s window. Note: navigator.clipboard.readText() is not reliable to read back in headless (needs transient activation), so the clipboard write was verified via the successful writeText resolve + the COPIED! feedback rather than a read-back. Console 0 errors / 0 warnings.
- Console: 0 errors / 0 warnings.
- Screenshots: .evidence/I159/I159_gameover_share_button.png (game-over: SCORE 50, KILLS 1 WAVE 1, MAX COMBO 1, ACCURACY 10%, PRESS ENTER TO RESTART, COPY RESULT button + 'copies a reproducible result code' caption).

## I160 - In-run HUD rework: compact score + inline multiplier (ST-160)
- Goal: make the in-run HUD calmer and more legible - the multiplier belongs with the score, and the bottom-center should not carry a big text block over the play area.
- Design (cgs-ux-design): Player Journey - during a chain the player glances top-left for score AND rate of gain in one place instead of jumping to bottom-center; HUD State - no chain: bare score line + hidden heat bar (status quo); chain active: inline xN.N beside the score (tier-colored, pops on kill/tier-up) + bottom-center heat bar (small COMBO label + 4s drain); chain broken/expired: both hidden again (same 0.15s fade); Input Path - unchanged (HUD is display-only); Accessibility - multiplier is text (label channel), tier color is additive only, font-scale --vr-fs respected, reduced-motion still suppresses the pop; Risks - losing the visible chain COUNT (accepted: the multiplier value already encodes the chain, and the kill feed + score popups carry kill feedback); scope stays in src/render/hud.js + GDD text.
- Changed files: src/render/hud.js (score row with #hud-mult; heatWrap with #hud-combo-label + #hud-combo-track; render() branch rewired, pop now on the multiplier, 1.15/1.35), design/gdd.md (HUD section: multiplier on score line, bottom-center heat bar).

Ship (idle headless) then took hits: onDamage() set combo 0 / lastKillTime -Infinity and on the next PLAYING frame the multiplier returned to display 'none' (heat wrap hidden) - the expiry/break path verified on the real render loop; the run ended GAME_OVER with the HUD hidden as usual (lives 0). ## I162 - E8D test batch: stats storage, hotkey contract, codec hardening (ST-162)
- Goal: ST-162 acceptance - 15+ new node --test tests for the E8D content (stats storage, hotkeys, codec). Skill: cgs-standards-test-standards (no cgs-tests skill exists in the library).
